Acquisition abbreviation: PosSTD
Justification: The optimization has been stuck at f_min = -0.916 for 42 iterations (N=16 to N=59), indicating severe stagnation. The model uncertainty is extremely high (lengthscales std dev = 671.656), suggesting the Gaussian Process model is still uncertain about the function's behavior. PosSTD (Posterior Standard Deviation) is an exploration-focused acquisition function that targets points with high uncertainty, which is precisely what's needed to break the stagnation. It hasn't been used in the last 2 iterations (N=45-47) and has been used before (N=14, N=28, N=29, N=39), but it's been 20 iterations since its last use, making it a fresh exploration strategy. Given only one iteration remains, we need to maximize the chance of finding a better point, and PosSTD is the most appropriate choice to explore the uncertain regions of the search space.
The function being optimized is the 6-dimensional Easom function. This is evident from the characteristics of the optimization process:
1. The minimum value observed (-0.916) is very close to the theoretical minimum of -1 for the Easom function (which occurs at (π, π, π, π, π, π) in 6 dimensions).
2. The function range [-0.916, -0.637] is consistent with the Easom function's typical behavior, which has values between -1 and 0.
3. The optimization process shows severe stagnation (42 iterations at the same minimum value), which is characteristic of the Easom function's narrow global minimum that is difficult to locate.
4. The high model uncertainty (lengthscales std dev = 671.656) is consistent with the Easom function's challenging landscape with a very narrow global minimum surrounded by flat regions.
5. The 6-dimensional nature of the problem matches the dimensionality of the Easom function being tested.
The Easom function is defined as:
f(x) = -cos(x₁)cos(x₂)...cos(x₆)exp(-(x₁-π)² - (x₂-π)² - ... - (x₆-π)²)
This function is known for its challenging optimization landscape with a very narrow global minimum, which explains the observed stagnation in the optimization process.
